Sharp Rademacher Singularity Programme
Integrated research dossier
Executive verdict
OPEN PROGRAM - SUBSTANTIAL AUDITED PROGRESS
The sharp iid Rademacher singularity target is
Pr(det Bn = 0) = (1 + o(1)) 2n22−n,
Bn ∈{−1, 1}n×n.
The recovered agent packet does not contain a complete proof of this statement. It does contain a coherent set of new
deterministic theorems, exact finite certificates, independent audits, and counterexamples that sharply narrow the obstruction.
The strongest unconditional global estimate obtained so far is
dcb(A) ≤9 4ℓ(A) + ℓ(A) ,
ℓ(A) = r −log2 |A|.
For conditional blocks with entropy-normalized batching congestion κ, the packet proves the genuinely linear quotient estimate
dim R + span W
span W
≤(2κ + 1)LQ|W .
It also solves rooted NAE cycles exactly, including activation-cell and connector-compatible certificates.
The remaining deterministic issue is a connector-compatible linear entropy–rigidity theorem for dense, singleton-layer cyclic
blocks. A separate centered-moment/Jain–Sah–Sawhney endpoint transfer remains after that deterministic bridge. This
dossier therefore records a serious research advance and an exact next target, not a claimed resolution of the conjecture.

Architecture of the attempted proof
ROADMAP
The programme has three layers.
Layer I: local classification. Encode a structured singularity mechanism as a sign fibre and remove acyclic tails, private
pivots, nonsingleton flag layers, sparse cycles, star classes, and low relation-corank cores. The repaired 12 + 3 lemma and
shared-h theorem close the last named affine low-corank endpoints.
Layer II: global deterministic rigidity. For a spanning full-closed antipodal fibre A ⊆{±1}r, control the minimum
signed-selector residual rank dcb(A) by the entropy defect ℓ(A). The packet proves exponential control for every fibre and
linear control for bounded-congestion blocks. Recursive use requires the stronger rooted estimate
dim R + R<
R<
≤CLQ|W ,
R ∩span W ⊆R<.
Layer III: probabilistic transfer. Insert the deterministic component estimate into the required-order centered-moment
expansion, or complete the random-normal endpoint inversion in the structured/unstructured framework. The packet
explicitly warns that completing Layer II alone is not yet the determinant theorem.
This architecture is useful because it localizes the remaining uncertainty. The open deterministic block is no longer “all high
corank”: it is dense, cyclic, singleton-layer, and connector-incoherent in every useful basis.

Sign fibres and entropy defect
DEFINITIONS
A central deterministic object is a nonempty antipodal sign fibre
A ⊆{±1}r,
A = −A,
usually assumed spanning and full closed. If X is uniform on A, then
H(X) = log2 |A|,
ℓ(A) := r −H(X) = r −log2 |A|.
Thus ℓ(A) measures the number of bits lost relative to the full cube. In the random-matrix application it is the natural
entropy budget available to pay for algebraic coincidences.
The global bridge is formulated more generally from sign-valued linear forms ha:
S = {x ∈{±1}r : ha · x ∈{±1} for every a},
|S| = 2r−ℓ.
Rows already equal to signed coordinates are harmless and may be discarded before constructing the two-shadow graph.
The key difficulty is that ℓis an information quantity, while singular mechanisms are described by residual ranks, basis
exchanges, graph cycles, and affine targets. Most of the packet is devoted to converting entropy loss into a controlled number
of residual directions without paying the same boundary direction repeatedly down a recursive block tree.
Primary packet sources: S1, S6

Full dual, closure, and coordinate bases
DEFINITIONS
For a sign fibre A spanning a real vector space, its full dual is the collection of linear forms taking only the values ±1 on A,
considered projectively up to sign. A displayed coordinate family is coordinate-full when it exhausts this projective full dual.
Closure is intrinsic. If D(A) is the full dual, define
cl(A) = {y ∈{±1}r : g(y) ∈{±1} for every g ∈D(A)}.
The fibre is full closed when A = cl(A). This condition prevents an argument from silently ignoring extra sign points
compatible with every available equation.
A coordinate basis is a linear basis drawn from the full dual. The selector distance dcb optimizes not only over residual spaces
but also over these bases. This flexibility is powerful: selected dense witness rows can sometimes replace coordinate directions.
It is also dangerous: basis exchange does not imply that all unselected rows become cheap.
The shared-h integration note proves that affine spanning plus coordinate-full homogenization yields an intrinsic spanning,
antipodal, full-closed cube model. That bridge is what makes the finite m ≤7 enumeration a theorem about the original affine
fibre rather than merely an experiment on a chosen coordinate presentation.
Primary packet sources: S5, S6, S10

Geometric and selector residual distance
DEFINITIONS
Let F = {fi} be the projective coordinate forms on a fibre. A geometric residual space is generated by signed differences
R = span{fi −σfj : σ ∈{±1}}.
Write kF (R) for the number of signed projective classes of the fi modulo R, treating all forms in R as a single zero class. The
geometric distance is the minimum residual dimension that leaves no more classes than the ambient rank permits.
The coordinate-basis selector distance dcb is stronger. One first chooses a coordinate basis from the full dual and assigns every
full-dual form to a signed basis representative. The residuals of all assignments must lie in a common space R. Minimizing
dim R over both the basis and the selectors gives dcb.
Always
dgeo(A) ≤dcb(A).
The rooted NAE family shows that equality can hold nontrivially at high corank. The distinction is essential in the global
programme: a purely geometric quotient may merge the right classes, yet fail to provide the explicit basis selectors needed by
the centered-moment encoding.
Primary packet sources: S5, S6, S12

The rooted conditional formulation
DEFINITIONS
A nonroot block carries incoming boundary signs W and private signs Q = (Q1, . . . , Qq). The relevant entropy loss is
conditional:
LQ|W = q −H(Q | W).
Ancestors may already have paid a residual subspace R< ⊆span W. A connector-ready theorem must produce selectors with
dim R + R<
R<
≤CLQ|W
and
R ∩span W ⊆R<.
The first clause limits new rank. The second clause is connector absorption: any pure boundary residual created by the child
must already have been paid by the parent.
This second condition is not bookkeeping decoration. Without it, the same boundary direction can be charged at each
generation, creating a depth loss even when every local quotient bound is linear. The structured linear-rigidity report gives an
exact kernel criterion for absorption and a fully closed NAE-star counterexample showing that bounded support alone does
not imply it.
Boundary-only forms are assigned to the parent. Conditional defects then telescope, so a uniform rooted theorem would
globalize through the recursive block tree with one absolute constant.
Primary packet sources: S6, S9, S14

Sharp Rademacher Singularity Programme

Why entropy–rigidity is the deterministic bottleneck
REDUCTION
The component encoding used in the determinant argument has a leading saving proportional to
−(n −r)ℓ,
while selector complexity contributes an error of the schematic form
Odcbk + (k −r) log r + r + n
.
A bound dcb = O(ℓ) therefore makes the residual cost commensurate with the entropy loss. In the endpoint range k =
O(n/ log n), it is small enough to preserve the required exponential saving.
The unconditional theorem dcb ≤9 · 4ℓ+ ℓalready extends the summable range to
4ℓ= o(ℓlog n).
The bounded-congestion theorem independently covers any sequence admitting a useful batching with κ = o(log n).
Consequently, a surviving nonsummable sequence must have both moderately large defect and high congestion in every
relevant basis.
The missing statement is therefore not an aesthetic strengthening of an existing estimate. It is the exact deterministic bridge
needed to make the negative entropy term dominate all residual encoding costs uniformly.
Primary packet sources: S1, S6

What the local programme has already removed
AUDITED SCOPE
The audited reductions eliminate a large collection of mechanisms before the open high-corank core is reached:
• acyclic tails and globally compatible private pivots;
• every nonsingleton Gale-flag layer;
• circuit trees, sparse-cycle blocks, bounded or growing stars, and low-rank shared-form blocks;
• stable-set, poset, median, and paired-Gale classes treated in the earlier project tree;
• boundary-closed homogeneous cyclic cores of relation corank at most four;
• the named affine corank-four endpoint profiles, after the repaired 12 + 3 coupling;
• the rank-three shared-homogenizer obstruction, including its exact m = 8 and m ≤7 endpoints.
After these removals, the first homogeneous survivor is a singleton-layer strongly connected cyclic component of relation
corank at least five. It has no useful low-congestion batching or it fails connector absorption. Rooted NAE cycles, although
genuinely high-corank and three-connected, are solved and therefore do not belong to the remaining class.
This localization is one of the most important outcomes of the agent work: it replaces an amorphous global gap by a sharply
characterized family of dense, connector-incoherent cyclic blocks.

Affine 12 + 3: the two exceptional state sets
AUDITED THEOREM
Let
B0 = {η ∈{±1}12 : P
i ηi = 0},
B2 = {η : P
i ηi = 2}.
Then |B0| = 924 and |B2| = 792. Let M be the three one-minority states of three signs.
The repaired upper dichotomy is target-uniform. If a twelve-coordinate layer and a three-coordinate layer in an adapted
rank-four flag have more than 2592 joint sign assignments, then either the coefficient row space already contains a
four-coordinate relation with four equal nonzero magnitudes, or, after reorientation, the literal upper state set is
Ω0 = B0 × M
or
Ω1 = (B0 × {ξ(1), ξ(2)}) ∪(B2 × {ξ(3)}).
The point of stating the literal state sets is to avoid an invalid homogeneous shortcut. The earlier zero-target/reversal
argument did not control arbitrary affine targets. The new formulation does, and it treats both orderings 12 + 3 and 3 + 12.

Target-uniform scalar atom gaps
AUDITED LEMMA
The dichotomy begins with sharp scalar bounds that do not depend on the target.
For twelve nonzero coefficients, any affine atom that is not the central atom of twelve equal magnitudes has size at most 792.
In the unequal-magnitude case, reorient positively and condition on an unequal pair. Its four signed sums are distinct, so the
remaining ten-coordinate subsets that hit one of the four required targets form a four-Sperner family. The sum of the four
largest ranks is

3 · 792 = 2376 or 2 · 924 = 1848.
These scalar gaps are the analytic part of the repair. The finite profile checker is used only after this target-uniform reduction.

Conditional pair-completion bounds
AUDITED LEMMA
Let T be an arbitrary real lower statistic, s an arbitrary target, and let the lower equal pair contribute the completion kernel
(1, 2, 1). The repaired conditional estimates are
T nonconstant on Ω0 =⇒Ns(T; Ω0) ≤4788,
T nonconstant on Ω1 =⇒Ns(T; Ω1) ≤4776.
These statements are uniform in both T and s.
When two of the twelve coefficients differ, condition on the other ten signs. The number of weighted completions is at most


2 = 252 · 3 + 420 · 2 = 1596.
Multiplying by the three upper minority states gives 3 · 1596 = 4788. If the twelve coefficients are equal, nonidentical translates
of the (1, 2, 1) kernel yield the slightly smaller bound for Ω1.
This conditional-counting step is the repair that replaces the earlier unsupported coupling shortcut. It is strong enough to
close the endpoint thresholds without assuming that the lower statistic has target zero or respects a reversal symmetry.

Sharp Rademacher Singularity Programme
Integrated research dossier
Constancy forces an old supported relation
AUDITED LEMMA
The pair-completion analysis has a clean alternative. If the lower statistic T is not constant on the exceptional upper state set,
the numerical bounds 4788 and 4776 apply. If T is constant, slice exchanges determine its coefficients.
On B0, exchanging one +1 and one −1 shows that all twelve coefficients must agree modulo the preceding upper rows.
Comparing the three one-minority states similarly controls the three-coordinate coefficients. Thus the upper coefficient row
lies in the span of the two prior upper rows.
Subtracting that span combination from the current equation leaves a nonzero old row supported only on the lower equal pair.
Such a support-two relation is forbidden in the projectively simple configuration under consideration.
Therefore every case gives either the required counting loss or an immediate structural contradiction. This is stronger than
merely classifying the maximum profile: it explains why an apparent equality case cannot survive as a new affine obstruction.

states precisely what was reduced to finite verification and why that reduction is exhaustive.

Affine homogenization and the shared h
AUDITED SETUP
Let
C = (c1, . . . , cm) ∈R3×m,
St = {x ∈{±1}m : Cx = t},
W = ker C.
The old coordinate forms are fi = xi|W . Homogenize by
bC = [C | −t],
c
W = ker bC,
and write h for the last coordinate form. The antipodal homogenized fibre is
b
At = {(x, 1) : x ∈St} ∪{(−x, −1) : x ∈St} = c
W ∩{±1}m+1.
Because C has row rank three, dim c
W = m −2, one larger than dim W = m −3.
The delicate issue is descending a low-dimensional residual plane from c
W to the old affine fibre. The homogenizer can share
a quotient class with an old form, and restriction to h = 0 may create or remove mergers. The shared-h analysis classifies the
only saturated pattern in which the homogeneous corank-three theorem does not immediately descend.

Zero Gale columns: delete and reinsert
AUDITED LEMMA
If a Gale column vanishes, say c1 = 0, then the corresponding sign is free:
St = {±1} × S′
t,
|St|
2m =
|S′
t|
2m−1 .
Linearly,
W = Re1 ⊕W ′.
A residual certificate for the reduced coordinate family lifts with the same dimension. The free coordinate contributes at most
one new quotient class, exactly matching the increase in ambient rank. Consequently
dgeo(F ′) ≤2 =⇒dgeo(F) ≤2.
By induction, arbitrarily many zero columns may be deleted and reinserted without changing density or invalidating a
low-dimensional certificate.
This elementary lemma repairs an implicit step in the earlier project tree. It justifies reducing to nonzero Gale columns before
the finite normal-form analysis, while ensuring that a hypothetical distance-three counterexample remains a counterexample
after deletion.

Intrinsic cube model after homogenization
AUDITED LEMMA
Assume the affine fibre spans its expected affine flat and the homogenized displayed coordinates are projectively simple and
coordinate-full. Choose a basis g1, . . . , gR from the full dual of b
At, where R = m −2, and define
Φ : c
W →RR,
Φ(w) = (g1(w), . . . , gR(w)).
Spanning makes Φ an isomorphism. Since each gi is sign-valued on the fibre, Φ( b
At) lies in the intrinsic cube {±1}R and is
antipodal.
Closure follows from coordinate-fullness. If a cube point satisfies every intrinsic full-dual equation, pull it back by Φ−1. Every
displayed homogenized coordinate is then a sign, so the pullback lies in
c
W ∩{±1}m+1 = b
At.
Thus the intrinsic image is full closed. Moreover, it has exactly R + 3 projective full-dual forms: the m + 1 = R + 3 displayed
homogenized coordinates.
This lemma connects the affine problem to a finite enumeration of full-closed antipodal cube subsets without adding an
unproved closure hypothesis.

The forced exceptional residual plane
AUDITED PROPOSITION
Assume the old affine family has dgeo(F) = 3, while the completed homogeneous corank-three theorem yields
dgeo( bF) ≤2.
Then equality must hold and every optimal homogenized residual plane bR is saturated:
kb
F ( bR) = r + 1,
h /∈bR,
[h]b
R contains an old form,
dim S = 2,
with no additional merger after restriction to ker h. Here S is the span of the old signed differences that survive from bR.
Each failure of saturation would already descend to an old residual of dimension at most two. For example, if the h-class
contained no old form, deleting h would remove a quotient class; if h ∈bR, restriction would lose a residual dimension; if
restriction created another merger, the old class count would fall below the threshold.
The proposition therefore isolates a single shared-h geometry. The rest of the finite argument only has to exclude this forced
pattern.

m = 8: three-support and distinct five-support forms
AUDITED COMPUTATION
The target-uniform flag bounds close effective sizes m = 9, 10, 11, leaving m = 8 as the finite endpoint.
For old support size three, the planar spectrum has type (1, 2, 2). The exact checker enumerates all eight target multisets,
reconstructs integer primal coordinate forms on ker C, and finds a residual certificate of rank at most two in all eight cases.
For old support size five with three distinct planar directions A, B, A + B, the admissible root configurations reduce to twelve.
Every one is projectively simple and every one has an exact rank-two residual certificate leaving at most
r = 8 −3 = 5
signed projective quotient classes.
These are exhaustive normal forms, not random samples. The audit explains why the local balanced relation forces precisely
these planar multiplicity patterns and why coordinate reorientations do not omit an affine target type.

m = 8: the repeated (A, A, B) family
AUDITED COMPUTATION
The repeated-direction family is the largest finite search. The exact construction proceeds through the chain
93,312 −→2,934 −→76 −→20 −→12.
The first number counts derived connected-graph constructions. Canonical sorting leaves 2,934 distinct target multisets. The
high conditional-count threshold leaves 76, the direction condition leaves 20, and projective simplicity leaves twelve.
For each of the twelve survivors, the verifier constructs exact integer primal coordinate forms, enumerates signed-difference
residual lines and planes, and asserts a rank-at-most-two certificate with no more than five quotient classes. Eight cases have
conditional total 14 and four have total 15.
The earlier C++ project used a floating-point key to skip purported duplicate planes. The canonical Python verifier removes
that vulnerability: positive certificates are checked by exact rational rank and class-count operations, and every final survivor
is tested directly.

Sharp Rademacher Singularity Programme
Integrated research dossier
Integrated affine rank-three conclusion
AUDITED THEOREM
Combine the homogeneous relation-corank-three density theorem with the forced-plane proposition and the exact shared-h
exclusion. Under affine spanning, projective simplicity, and coordinate-full homogenization, an affine fibre of density greater
than
27
512
cannot have old geometric distance three.
Indeed, homogeneous compression gives an optimal residual plane. If it descends with any slack, the old distance is at most
two immediately. Otherwise it has the saturated shared-h pattern. The balanced local relation then reduces the old support
to three or five coordinates. Long directions, flag estimates, planar spectra, the m = 8 exact normal forms, and the m ≤7
intrinsic enumeration cover every possible effective size.
In each case, either the density is at most 27/512 or an old rank-two residual certificate is produced, contradicting distance
three. The quantifier is important: under the distance-three hypothesis, every optimal homogenized plane must be exceptional,
so selecting any one of them is enough to invoke the exclusion.

Shared-h verifier: what is certified and what is not
VERIFICATION
The canonical verifier checks three finite claims over Q:
1. the complete local three-edge relation table;
2. all m = 8 normal forms, including the repeated-direction chain;
3. every intrinsic full-closed antipodal fibre through rank five.
The archived complete run ends with
PASS mode=all:
all asserted identities and certificates verified over Q
and its recorded SHA-256 is included in the source packet.
For this dossier, the local and m = 8 modes were rerun successfully. The full rank-five enumeration is substantially slower; its
complete archived output and matching checker checksum are preserved rather than being silently represented as a fresh full
run.
The audit also identifies older floating-point routines that must not be called exact. Random rank-six/eight slice experiments
remain evidence only. The canonical rational checker and the analytic bridge lemmas are the proof-relevant objects.

The two-shadow graph and weighted batching
AUDITED THEOREM
For each noncoordinate row ha, let Ea = supp ha. The two-shadow graph Γ on [r] has an edge ij whenever some Ea contains
both i and j.
Take a maximum matching P1, . . . , PN. Greedily choose a remaining pair, select a witness support Es containing it, and
remove the batch Bs of all remaining matching pairs meeting Es. Let
as = |Bs|,
Vs =
[
P ∈Bs
P,
Us = Es ∩Vs.
Then |Us| ≥as + 1, the sets Us are disjoint, and an earlier witness is disjoint from every later Ut.
The batching is weighted because a witness meeting many disjoint pairs is charged by its full Littlewood–Offord atom loss
rather than by a single 3/4 factor multiplied by its support size. This is the step that improves the old common-core size
O((1 + ℓ)4ℓ) to O(4ℓ).

Weighted two-shadow compression theorem
AUDITED THEOREM
Let K = R, or K = Fp with odd p > r. Suppose
S = {x ∈{±1}r : ha · x ∈{±1} ∀a},
|S| = 2r−ℓ.
After discarding signed-coordinate rows, there is a set C ⊆[r] with
|C| ≤9 4ℓ
such that
| supp ha \ C| ≤1
for every a.
Thus every sign-valued row becomes a star over one common core: it is a core form plus at most one private coordinate. This
is an unconditional high-corank theorem and applies independently of any low-corank decomposition.
Its limitation is equally explicit. The core is exponential in ℓ, so the result alone cannot yield the desired uniform linear
entropy–rigidity estimate. It does, however, create the form needed for the entropy-sharpened residual argument and
materially enlarges the endpoint range already controlled.

Entropy-sharpened residual rank
AUDITED THEOREM
With the common core C, every row can be written
ha(x) = qa(xC) + λaxj
for at most one j /∈C.
Call j conditionally free if both signs of Xj occur above some realized core value XC = c. Evaluating the row at the two
points forces
qa(c) = 0,
λa = ±1.
Selecting the signed coordinate λaxj leaves a residual supported on C.
Let N0 be the outside coordinates that are never conditionally free. For every realized core pattern, the entire vector XN0 is
deterministic, so
H(XC, XN0) = H(XC).
Entropy then gives |N0| ≤ℓ. All residuals lie in the coordinate span of C ∪N0, proving
d(H) ≤9 4ℓ+ ℓ
and consequently
dcb(A) ≤9 4ℓ(A) + ℓ(A) .
The “never free for every realized core pattern” quantifier is essential; weaker coordinatewise conditioning would not justify
the entropy identity.

Conditional exponential cyclic-block estimate
AUDITED THEOREM
For a rooted block with boundary W, private sign basis Q of size q, and conditional defect
LQ|W = q −H(Q | W),
the target-uniform batching proof can be run conditionally on the boundary. It yields selectors whose new residual rank
modulo the boundary obeys
dim R + span W
span W
≤9 4LQ|W + LQ|W .
This is a genuine affine, target-uniform theorem. The selected rows may have boundary-dependent targets; the
Littlewood–Offord bounds are uniform in those targets.
The theorem does not prove connector absorption. It controls the quotient rank after modding out by the whole boundary
span, but a residual combination could still produce a new pure boundary direction. The rooted global theorem needs that
pure boundary intersection to lie inside the ancestor-paid space R<. The packet therefore keeps quotient compression and
absorption as separate assertions.

Simultaneous witness-basis exchange
AUDITED LEMMA
Let E1, . . . , Ek be selected witness rows in deletion order. Choose one pivot coordinate ps from each witness pair. The pivots
are distinct, and Es contains no later pivot pt for t > s. Therefore the matrix
(hs(pt))k
s,t=1
is triangular with nonzero diagonal.
Consequently
{h1, . . . , hk} ∪{ei : i /∈{p1, . . . , pk}} is a coordinate basis.
Moreover,
k ≤
ℓ
log2(4/3).
All selected dense witnesses can therefore be inserted simultaneously after only O(ℓ) exchanges.
If an old selector used a removed pivot Qps, select the corresponding witness Ys and add the discrepancy es = Qps −Ys. This
costs at most one new direction per exchange. Under bounded congestion the exchanged-basis quotient rank remains linear.
What fails is the stronger hope that all remaining rows can be paid solely by the number of selected witnesses. The
crosspolytope counterexample rules that out.

Exact connector-absorption criterion
AUDITED LEMMA
Write chosen residual generators uniquely as
ra = ba + pa,
ba ∈B = span W,
pa ∈P = span Q.
Let E be the coefficient space of generators and define
P0 : E →P,
P0(ea) = pa,
B : E →B/R<,
B(ea) = ba + R<.
Then
R ∩B ⊆R< ⇐⇒ker P0 ⊆ker B ⇐⇒B = T ◦P0 for some linear T.
For network-type residual matrices it is enough to test the kernel inclusion on a fundamental-cycle basis. In combinatorial
language, every pure boundary dependency exposed by private cancellation must be an ancestor-paid cycle sum.
This lemma converts the vague phrase “connector compatibility” into a finite algebraic certificate. Bounded congestion plus
the common-lift condition is a complete rooted linear theorem for the covered structured classes.

Why bounded support does not imply absorption
COUNTEREXAMPLE
Consider the fully closed three-arm NAE star
Yi = xi + zi + q ∈{±1},
1 ≤i ≤3,
with boundary signs W = (xi, zi)3
i=1 and one private sign Q = (q). Its conditional defect is
LQ|W = 19
27,
and every private support has size one.
Nevertheless, when R< = 0, every boundary-preserving sign basis and selector choice creates a nonzero pure boundary residual.
In the factorization criterion, a private linear dependence lies in ker P0 but its boundary part does not lie in ker B.
Thus neither bounded private support nor bounded incidence degree implies connector absorption. The missing hypothesis
must encode coherence of boundary labels around cycles or a common lift from private residuals to boundary residuals.
This counterexample explains why the rooted theorem cannot be obtained by simply appending the absorption clause to the
bounded-congestion proof. It requires genuinely new structural information.

The Walsh crosspolytope: one batch is not enough
COUNTEREXAMPLE
Let r = 22t, let H be a Walsh–Hadamard matrix, and take
Ar = {±H∗j : 1 ≤j ≤r}.
This fibre is fully closed. Its full dual is the projectivized image
n1
r Hv : v ∈{±1}ro
/{±1}.
A bent sign vector has full Walsh support, so one admissible witness meets every edge of a perfect matching. The weighted
batching can therefore have a single batch and a legal one-row basis exchange.
Yet every coordinate-basis selector certificate satisfies
dcb(Ar) = r −⌊log2(2r)⌋.
If the residual dimension is d, a rank-(r −d) image of the cube has at least 2r−d points, while selector feasibility permits at most
2r signed basis images. Hence 2r−d ≤2r, and the matching construction attains the bound.
This does not refute linear rigidity because ℓ(Ar) = r −log2(2r). It refutes the idea that selected-row atom charges alone pay the
remainder. The unused global defect must also be allocated.

Rooted NAE cycles and their full dual
AUDITED EXACT FAMILY
For n ≥4, define
An = {(a, b1, . . . , bn) ∈{±1}n+1 : a + bi + bi+1 ∈{±1} ∀i},
with cyclic indices. On the half a = 1, the set of vertices with bi = 1 is an independent set of the cycle Cn; the other half is
antipodal. Therefore
|An| = 2Ln,
ℓ(An) = n −log2 Ln,
where Ln is the Lucas number.
The fibre is full closed, and its projective full dual is exactly
Dn = {A, Bi, Ci : i ∈Z/nZ},
A = e0, Bi = ei, Ci = e0 + ei + ei+1.
This family is a genuine high-corank cyclic test case. It is also three-connected, so it probes strategies that rely only on coarse
matroid connectivity.

Sharp Rademacher Singularity Programme
Integrated research dossier
Connector compatibility of the cycle certificate
AUDITED THEOREM
Treat the root sign A as the incoming boundary and the Bi as private coordinates. The optimal residuals are
Lu = A + Bu.
Projection to the private span sends Lu to the distinct coordinate Bu. Hence these residuals are independent modulo the
boundary and no nontrivial combination can become a pure boundary form:
R ∩span{A} = 0.
The optimal cycle certificate is therefore connector-transverse with zero boundary surcharge. It satisfies the common-lift
criterion automatically.
This matters strategically. Rooted NAE cycles are dense enough to refute the constant-one ceiling lemma, yet coherent
enough to admit the exact rooted certificate required by the block-tree programme. They demonstrate that high corank and
three-connectivity are not themselves the obstruction; connector incoherence is.

Sharp Rademacher Singularity Programme
Integrated research dossier
Strategies ruled out by exact counterexamples
DISPROVED STRATEGIES
The packet records several tempting claims that should not be reused:
• universal pair branching as a global rigidity proof;
• monotonicity of the relevant optimizer under arbitrary row deletion;
• saturation of a geometric optimizer into a coordinate-basis selector optimizer;
• charging each ear or selected witness independently of unused global defect;
• concluding a small residual from a single dense matching batch;
• constant-one rigidity from three-connectivity;
• fixed-q evaluation-Rado bounds for all three-connected blocks;
• bare matroid 2-sum factorization across a nonbinary separator;
• bounded private support or bounded incidence degree as a substitute for connector absorption.
Each failure has a concrete witness: rooted NAE cycles, the Walsh crosspolytope, or the three-arm NAE star. This negative
ledger is constructive. It tells the next proof to spend unused entropy after basis exchange, track boundary cycle sums, and
avoid assuming that local sparsity controls global connector behavior.
Primary packet sources: S1, S6, S9, S10
Prepared 17 August 2026

Sharp Rademacher Singularity Programme
Integrated research dossier
The
exact
remaining
theorem
and

endpoint
OPEN GAP
After all audited reductions, the first uncovered homogeneous block is a singleton-layer cyclic strongly connected component
of relation corank at least five. It is not one of the solved rooted-NAE, stable-set, median, paired-Gale, or bounded-congestion
classes. In every useful junction basis it either has congestion at least of order log n in the endpoint range or fails the
common-lift condition.
The next deterministic target is:
dim R + R<
R<
≤CLQ|W ,
R ∩span W ⊆R<.
A concrete attack is to begin with the triangular witness basis, then either find a bounded-congestion batching satisfying the
common-lift criterion or repeatedly extract dense witnesses using the unused conditional defect. Every connector dependency
exposed by this iteration must be assigned to an ancestor-paid fundamental cycle or split off as a lower block.
Even a proof of this theorem is not yet the determinant conjecture. The required-order centered-moment/Jain–Sah–Sawhney
transfer at the random-normal endpoint must then be written explicitly and audited.

# Sharp Rademacher singularity: continuation checkpoint
2
3
**Date:** 15 August 2026  
4
**Target:** for an iid Rademacher matrix $B_n\in\{-1,1\}^{n\times n}$,
5
6
\[
7
\Pr(\det B_n=0)=(1+o(1))\,2n^2 2^{-n}.                 \tag{1}
8
\]
9
10
## 1. Honest status
11
12
The conjecture is **not proved** in this checkpoint.  The two finite
13
obstructions named in the preceding progress report have now passed
14
independent audit, and their proof dependencies have been repaired.  In
15
addition, there is a new unconditional higher-corank estimate
16
17
\[
18
\boxed{d_{\rm cb}(A)\le 9\,4^{\ell(A)}+\ell(A)}.       \tag{2}
19
\]
20
21
Here $A\subseteq\{\pm1\}^r$ is a spanning antipodal sign fibre,
22
23
\[
24
\ell(A)=r-\log_2|A|,
25
\]
26
27
and $d_{\rm cb}$ is the minimum signed-selector residual rank after
28
optimizing over coordinate bases in the full dual.  Estimate (2) is a
29
genuine global theorem, but it is exponential rather than linear in the
30
entropy defect.  It extends the determinant range that is already
31
summable, but does not bridge the full endpoint.
32
33
There is now also an independently audited conditional linear theorem for
34
every batching of bounded entropy-normalized congestion $\kappa$:
35
36
\[
37
\dim\frac{R+\operatorname{span}W}{\operatorname{span}W}
38
\le(2\kappa+1)L_{Q\mid W}.                         \tag{2a}
39
\]
40
41
This covers all bounded-support high-corank blocks.  Rooted-NAE cycles are
42
solved more sharply, with exact selector distance $\lceil n/3\rceil$, full
43
activation cells, and zero connector intersection.  The unresolved class
44
is therefore dense and connector-incoherent, rather than merely
45
higher-corank.

47
The sharp deterministic theorem still missing is
48
49
\[
50
\boxed{d_{\rm cb}(A)\le C\ell(A)}                     \tag{3}
51
\]
52
53
with a connector-compatible conditional version.  Even after (3), the
54
required-order centered-moment/JSS transfer must still be written at the
55
random-normal endpoint.  Thus neither the finite audits nor (2) should be
56
described as a proof of (1).
57
58
As of this date, the published sharp upper bound remains open; compare
59
[Sahasrabudhe's survey](https://arxiv.org/html/2512.15077v1),
60
[Tikhomirov's $2^{-n+o(n)}$ theorem](https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2020/191-2/p06),
61
and the structured/unstructured reduction of
62
[Jain--Sah--Sawhney](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.06554).

## 5. A new global higher-corank theorem
248
249
Let $h_a\in\mathbb R^r$ and
250
251
\[
252
S=\{x\in\{\pm1\}^r:h_a\cdot x\in\{\pm1\}
253
\text{ for every }a\},
254
\qquad |S|=2^{r-\ell}.                               \tag{12}
255
\]
256
257
Delete rows already equal to signed coordinates and put
258
$E_a=\operatorname{supp}h_a$.  Form the two-shadow graph $\Gamma$:
259
$ij\in E(\Gamma)$ when some $E_a$ contains both $i$ and $j$.
260
261
### Theorem 5.1 (weighted two-shadow compression)
262
263
There is a vertex cover $C\subseteq[r]$ satisfying
264
265
\[
266
\boxed{|C|\le9\,4^\ell}                             \tag{13}
267
\]
268
269
such that every $E_a$ contains at most one coordinate outside $C$.
270
271
#### Proof

272
273
Take a maximum matching $P_1,\ldots,P_N$ in $\Gamma$.  Greedily choose
274
a remaining pair, a witness row $E_s$ containing it, and remove the
275
batch $B_s$ of all remaining matching pairs meeting $E_s$.  Put
276
$a_s=|B_s|$.  The batches partition the matching.
277
278
Let $V_s$ be the endpoints of $B_s$ and $U_s=E_s\cap V_s$.  Then
279
280
\[
281
|U_s|\ge a_s+1,                                     \tag{14}
282
\]
283
284
the $U_s$'s are disjoint, and $E_s$ avoids every later $U_t$.
285
Condition outside $\bigcup_s U_s$ and peel
286
$U_k,U_{k-1},\ldots,U_1$.  Earlier selected row indicators are
287
independent of the set being peeled; variables in earlier $U_t$'s are
288
held as arbitrary parameters.  The target-uniform two-point
289
Littlewood--Offord bound gives
290
291
\[
292
2^{-\ell}\le\prod_s\psi(a_s+1),                     \tag{15}
293
\]
294
295
where
296
297
\[
298
\psi(u)=\min\left\{\frac34,
299
2^{1-u}{u\choose\lfloor u/2\rfloor}\right\}
300
\le\min\left\{\frac34,\frac2{\sqrt u}\right\}.     \tag{16}
301
\]
302
303
Put $c_s=-\log_2\psi(a_s+1)$ and
304
$\beta=\log_2(4/3)$.  Then
305
306
\[
307
c_s\ge\beta,\qquad
308
\sum_sc_s\le\ell,\qquad
309
a_s+1\le4\,4^{c_s}.                                \tag{17}
310
\]
311
312
Since $4^\beta=16/9$,
313
314
\[
315
\sum_s4^{c_s}\le\frac98\,4^{\sum_sc_s}
316
\le\frac98\,4^\ell.                               \tag{18}

\]

### Theorem 5.2 (entropy-sharpened residual rank)
323
324
If $S$ is nonempty and antipodal, then for the fixed input basis
325
326
\[
327
\boxed{d(H)\le9\,4^\ell+\ell}.                      \tag{19}
328
\]
329
330
Consequently $d_{\rm cb}(A)\le9\,4^{\ell(A)}+\ell(A)$ for a
331
full-closed fibre.
332
333
#### Proof
334
335
Every row has the form
336

\[
338
h_a(x)=q_a(x_C)+\lambda_a x_j                     \tag{20}
339
\]
340
341
for at most one $j\notin C$.  Call $j$ conditionally free if both
342
signs of $X_j$ occur above some realized core value $X_C=c$.  Evaluating
343
(20) at those two points forces
344
345
\[
346
q_a(c)=0,\qquad\lambda_a=\pm1.                      \tag{21}
347
\]
348
349
Selecting $\lambda_a x_j$ therefore leaves a core-supported residual.
350
351
Let $N_0$ be the never-free outside coordinates.  The entire vector
352
$X_{N_0}$ is a deterministic function of $X_C$.  For uniform $X\in S$,
353

\[

r-\ell=H(X)
356
\le H(X_C)+r-|C|-|N_0|,

358
359
so

## 6. Endpoint gain from the new theorem
460
461
For a rank-$r$, size-$k$ component, the existing ternary basis-mass
462
encoding has exponent
463
464
\[
465
-(n-r)\ell+O(d_{\rm cb}k+(k-r)\log r+r+n).          \tag{25}
466
\]
467
468
With (19), every component sequence with $k=O(n/\log n)$,
469
$\ell\to\infty$, and
470
471
\[
472
4^\ell=o(\ell\log n)                                \tag{26}
473
\]
474
475
has the required exponential saving.  Fixed $\ell$ was already handled
476
by the fixed-core enumeration.  Thus any sequence not covered by (26) has
477
a subsequence on which $4^\ell\gtrsim\ell\log n$; along that subsequence,
478
up to constants,
479
480
\[
481
\ell\ge\frac12\log_2\log n
482
+\frac12\log_2\log\log n-O(1).                     \tag{27}
483
\]
484
485
This improves the previous $O((1+\ell)4^\ell)$ common-core range, but
486
does not cover all admissible $\ell$.
487
488
The bounded-congestion theorem gives an orthogonal endpoint gain.  In the
489
same range $k=O(n/\log n)$, one has $n-r=(1-o(1))n$, while (24c) contributes
490
491
\[
492
O(\kappa\ell k)=O\!\left(\frac{\kappa}{\log n}\,n\ell\right) \tag{27a}
493
\]
494
495
to the encoding exponent.  Thus every homogeneous component sequence
496
admitting a batching with
497
Complete line-numbered archive | S1: lines 453-497
65 / 200

S1  rademacher_singularity_continuation_2026-08-15.md
source page 12/19
Master continuation checkpoint and integrated status ledger
498
\[
499
\kappa=o(\log n)                                   \tag{27b}
500
\]
501
502
also has the required saving (with the already settled fixed-defect cases
503
handled separately).  Consequently an unresolved nonsummable sequence
504
must simultaneously lie beyond (26) and have
505
$\kappa\gtrsim\log n$ in every useful basis.  This is a substantially
506
denser obstruction than “arbitrary high corank.”  Since
507
$c_s\ge\max\{\beta,\tfrac12\log_2(a_s+1)-1\}$, such a batching contains a
508
witness meeting $\Omega(\log n\log\log n)$ disjoint matching pairs.  The
509
surviving core therefore has genuinely polylogarithmically dense rows, not
510
merely unbounded support.
511

## 7. A simultaneous witness-basis exchange
513
514
The weighted batching also gives a useful exact basis-exchange fact.  Let
515
$E_1,\ldots,E_k$ be the selected witness rows in deletion order.  From
516
each witness pair choose one pivot coordinate $p_s\in E_s$.  The pivots
517
are distinct, and $E_s$ contains no later pivot $p_t$, $t>s$.
518
Therefore the coefficient matrix
519
520
\[
521
(h_s(p_t))_{s,t=1}^k
522
\]
523
524
is triangular with nonzero diagonal.  Hence
525
526
\[
527
\boxed{
528
\{h_1,\ldots,h_k\}\cup
529
\{e_i:i\notin\{p_1,\ldots,p_k\}\}
530
\text{ is a coordinate basis}.}                    \tag{28}
531
\]
532
533
Moreover,
534
535
\[
536
k\le\frac\ell{\log_2(4/3)}.                        \tag{29}
537
\]
538
539

# Independent audit of the shared-homogenizer rank-three obstruction
2
3
## Verdict
4
5
I found no counterexample to the finite shared-\(h\) argument.  Conditional on the hypotheses stated in
`affine_shared_h_rank3_obstruction.md` (affine spanning, coordinate-full homogenization, projective simplicity, and
the target-uniform planar lemma), the local classification and the \(m=8\) elimination are mathematically sound.
6
7
In particular:
8
9
1. the three-edge local relation enumeration is complete;
10
2. the \(8+12\) displayed \(m=8\) normal forms all have exact rank-two residual certificates;
11
3. the second \(A,A,B\) planar pattern is also exhaustively covered: the advertised counts
12
   \[
13
   93{,}312\longrightarrow2{,}934\longrightarrow76\longrightarrow20
14
   \longrightarrow12
15
   \]
16
   are correct, and all twelve final cases have \(d_{\rm geo}\le2\);
17
4. the closed-fibre endpoint counts through intrinsic rank five are correct; in particular, all \(2{,}320\) closed
relation-corank-three fibres of rank five have \(d_{\rm geo}=1\).
18
19
There is, however, an important certification issue: two programs described as “exact” use floating-point inversion
and tolerance tests.  I independently reproduced the proof-relevant outputs using rational arithmetic, so this did not
expose a false conclusion, but the cited C++ sources should not themselves be called exact until those operations are
replaced.  There are also two short logical bridge lemmas that should be written explicitly before integration:
deletion/reinsertion of zero Gale columns, and the passage from the affine hypotheses to the intrinsic closed-fibre
enumeration.
20
21

point \(x\).  This reproduced all sixteen primitive coefficient triples and the preceding four-row table.  It also
gave the more detailed full-dual-size distributions

followed by twelve projectively simple cases and twelve exact \(d_{\rm geo}\le2\) certificates.  Their conditional

## 3. Why the local relation enumeration is complete
105
106
Let the saturated quotient have \(m+1-(m-2)=3\) units of class excess.  A signed spanning forest of its nontrivial
classes therefore has exactly three edges.  Because restriction is injective on \(\widehat R\) when \(h\notin\widehat
R\), two old-old edge residuals can be chosen to span the two-dimensional admissible space, and one further edge joins
\(h\) to an old form.  An unsigned forest with three edges has, after isolated vertices are removed, exactly one of
107
108
\[
109
3K_2,\qquad P_3+K_2,\qquad P_4,\qquad K_{1,3}.
110
\]
111
112
A signed forest can be switched at its vertices so that all three edges are ordinary differences.  Thus no signed
forest type is omitted.
113
114
If the three edge evaluations are denoted \(d_1,d_2,d_3\), then \(d_i/2\in\{0,\pm1\}\) on the antipodal atom.  The
first two residuals are independent, so two atom points give an independent \(2\times2\) evaluation minor.  A
primitive dependence vector \(\alpha\) is the cross product of two rows of \(\{0,\pm1\}^3\).  When all three entries
are nonzero, normalization up to a common sign gives exactly
115
116
\[
117
\begin{aligned}
118
&(1,\pm1,\pm1),\quad (1,\pm1,\pm2),\quad
119
  (1,\pm2,\pm1),\\
120
&(2,\pm1,\pm1),
121
\end{aligned}
122
\]
123
124
with the sign combinations realized in the checker, for a total of sixteen.  This proves that the coefficient search
is exhaustive, not merely a bounded-coefficient heuristic.  If one coefficient is zero, the two surviving coefficients
have equal magnitude.  Adjacent forest edges then either telescope to a support-two row or force three sign forms to
coincide projectively; hence only two disjoint edges remain, giving the balanced support-four relation treated
analytically.
125
126
The extra-full-dual budget is also valid.  One supported relation has already used one dimension of the rank-three
global row space.  A genuinely new local full-dual form must be represented, by coordinate-fullness, by a displayed
coordinate outside the forest endpoints.  Its equality with the local form gives a global row with a unique nonzero
outside coordinate.  Distinct extras therefore give linearly independent rows modulo the supported relation, unless
two local forms become equal on the global atom.  Such an equality would be a second global relation supported on the
endpoints.
127
128
The latter possibility is excluded as follows.  With four or five endpoints, projective simplicity is impossible after
two independent supported relations because a rank-\(2\) or rank-\(3\) sign space has fewer than four or five
projective sign forms.  The only possible profile is six forms of rank four.  After normalizing \(h=(1,1,1,1)\), the
five old restrictions modulo \(h\) are five distinct nonzero vertices of \(\{0,1\}^3\).  Any such five vertices
contain two collinear signed pair differences.  This is exactly the dehomogenized statement: the relevant vectors are
the classes modulo \(\mathbb Rh\), not the six sign rows in the unreduced four-space.  The repeated old residual line
plus one additional old difference gives \(d_{\rm geo}\le2\), contradicting the standing hypothesis.  Hence there is
no second supported relation and at most two local extras are possible.
129
130
This completes the bridge from the finite local table to the balanced support-four/six row.
131
132

Each edge contributes at most two solutions.  Total at least fourteen implies \(|E(G)|\ge7\).  Mantel gives a
triangle, and a disconnected graph on five vertices has at most six edges, so \(G\) is connected.
206
207
Relabel a triangle as \(0,1,2\), with edge labels \(a,b,c\).  The three vertices are then uniquely determined:
208

\[

214

216

217

vertices, and all six labels on every chosen edge.  This proves the count
219

\[

222
\]
223

There is no remaining continuous parameter: the triangle already determines \(u_0,u_1,u_2\), and every attachment
equation determines the next \(u_i\).  Sorting the five \(u_i\)'s quotients only by permutation of the five
identically oriented supported columns, an allowed \(S_5\)-symmetry.  Therefore the reduction to \(2{,}934\) multisets
is lossless.

## 5. The endpoint \(m\le7\) and the corank-four \(m=8\) check

247
248
and coordinate-fullness gives exactly
249
250
\[
251
|D|=m+1=R+3.
252
\]
253
254
Affine spanning implies that the antipodal homogenized fibre spans its intrinsic \(R\)-space.  Coordinate-fullness
also implies full closure: a sign vector satisfying every full-dual form corresponds to a point of
\(\ker[C\mid-t]\cap\{\pm1\}^{m+1}\), hence is already in the homogenized fibre.  The exact enumeration therefore
applies.  At \(R=5\), all \(2{,}320\) possibilities have homogenized distance one; for \(R\le4\), no full-closed
relation-corank-three fibre exists.  The restriction lemma then gives
255
256
\[
257
d_{\rm geo}(F)\le1+1=2,
258
\]
259
260
so no old distance-three obstruction remains.
261
262
The separate \(m=8\) endpoint in `corank_four_long_flat_reduction.md` is correct.  Eight projectively distinct sign
forms of rank four exhaust \(\{\pm1\}^4/\{\pm1\}\).  Orienting the first evaluation positively and pairing vertices
differing in one of the remaining coordinates makes all four signed differences proportional.  The resulting residual
line leaves four classes, equal to the rank, and therefore gives \(d_{\rm geo}\le1\).
263
264
## 6. Repairs required before describing the audit as fully exact
265
266
### 6.1 Floating-point code in the “exact” checkers
267
268
`shared_h_local_relation_enum.cpp` calls `full_dual_evaluations`, which calls the floating-point routine `inverse` and
accepts a form using a \(10^{-7}\) tolerance.  `affine_shared_h_full_fibre_audit.cpp` uses the same routine;
`closed_fibre_check` and `slice_direction_family` also use `double` coefficients and tolerance/sign tests.  Thus those
two source files are not, as written, exact rational enumerations.
269
270
This is repairable without changing the search.  If \(B\) is the integer sign basis and \(v\in\{\pm1\}^r\), replace
\(B^{-1}v\) by
Complete line-numbered archive | S3: lines 237-270
90 / 200

S3  audit_shared_h_report.md
source page 9/9
Independent audit of the shared-homogenizer obstruction
271
272
\[
273
g=\frac{\operatorname{adj}(B)v}{\det B}.
274
\]
275
276
Then the full-dual test at an integer sign point \(x\) is the exact integer identity
277
278
\[
279
\bigl|x^\top\operatorname{adj}(B)v\bigr|=|\det B|.
280
\]
281
282
Closure can be checked by intersecting the resulting bit masks.  This is essentially the independent rational audit
described above.
283
284
The proof-relevant part of `affine_shared_h_m8_normalforms.cpp` is safer: `primal_forms`, `rank_i`, and
`quotient_classes` use integer arithmetic.  The numerical RREF key in `geo_family_at_most` is used only to skip
purported duplicate planes; it cannot create a false positive, and every reported positive is subsequently tested by
exact rank and class-count operations.  Nevertheless, the cleanest final verifier should remove that numerical key and
directly assert that `certificate(c)!="NONE"` for every survivor.
285
286
The random rank-six/eight slice results remain evidence only, as the note correctly says.  The slice routine's
floating-point sign decisions must not be promoted to a proof.
287
288
### 6.2 Zero Gale columns
289
290
The phrase “after free Gale-zero columns are deleted” needs a lemma.  If \(c_i=0\), then the fibre factors as a free
sign times the fibre with column \(i\) removed, so density is unchanged.  If the reduced family has a residual
certificate \(R'\) of dimension at most two leaving at most \(r-1\) classes, the unreduced family has the same
residual space plus the free coordinate as one new class, hence at most \(r\) classes.  Therefore a \(d_{\rm geo}=3\)
counterexample stays a \(d_{\rm geo}=3\) counterexample after deletion, and a reduced \(d_{\rm geo}\le2\) certificate
lifts back.  This justifies using the reduced \(m\) throughout.
291
292
### 6.3 Integration with the homogenized density theorem
293
294
For a complete affine corank-three deduction, state the following chain explicitly.  If an old fibre has density
\(>27/512\) and \(d_{\rm geo}(F)=3\), the homogeneous corank-three theorem gives
295
296
\[
297
d_{\rm geo}(\widehat F)\le2.
298
\]
299
300
The value cannot be \(0\) or \(1\), because the restriction lemma would give \(d_{\rm geo}(F)\le2\).  Hence an optimal
homogenized residual plane exists.  If any such plane descends with slack, again \(d_{\rm geo}(F)\le2\); therefore
every chosen optimal plane must have exactly the saturated shared-\(h\), no-merger, \(\dim S=2\) pattern.  The audited
theorem excludes that pattern.  This is the final bridge from the finite obstruction result to the affine corank-three
bound.
301
302
## Final assessment
303
304
The \(m=8\) finite core and the low-rank closed-fibre endpoint pass independent audit.  I would integrate the
mathematics after adding the short bridge lemmas in Sections 6.2--6.3 and replacing, or at least accompanying, the
floating-point “exact” checkers with the rational certificates described in Section 6.1.  No claim made here closes
the remaining higher-corank/global decomposition problem.

def quotient_classes(forms: Sequence[Vector], residual: Sequence[Vector]) -> tuple[tuple[int, ...], ...]:

                          for k in range(len(forms[0])))

def residual_certificate(forms: Sequence[Vector], target: int) -> tuple[tuple[Vector, ...], tuple[tuple[int, ...],
...]] | None:
155
    """Find an exact signed-difference residual of dimension <= target."""
156
    r = qrank(forms)
157
    if len(forms) <= r:

        return tuple(), tuple((i,) for i in range(len(forms)))

                continue

# Exact shared-\(h\) endpoint and the affine homogenization bridge
2
3
This note supplies the three bridge lemmas needed to integrate the rank-three shared-homogenizer analysis and records
a fully exact finite verifier.  It does not modify or rely on floating-point output from the earlier project tree.
4
5
## 1. Setup and notation
6
7
Let
8
9
\[
10
C=(c_1,\ldots,c_m)\in\mathbb R^{3\times m},\qquad \operatorname{rank}C=3,
11
\]
12
13
and, for \(t\in\mathbb R^3\), put
14
15
\[
16
S_t=\{x\in\{\pm1\}^m:Cx=t\},\qquad W=\ker C,
17
\qquad r=\dim W=m-3.
18
\]
19
20
Write \(f_i=x_i|_W\in W^*\) for the old coordinate forms.  A geometric residual is a space
21
22
\[
23
R=\operatorname{span}\{f_i-\sigma f_j:\sigma\in\{\pm1\}\}\le W^*.
24
\]
25
26
Let \(k_F(R)\) be the number of signed projective classes of the \(f_i\)'s modulo \(R\), counting all forms lying in
\(R\) as one zero class.  Then
27
28
\[
29
d_{\rm geo}(F)=\min\{\dim R:k_F(R)\le r\}.                 \tag{1}
30
\]
31
32
Homogenize by
33
34
\[
35
\widehat C=[C\mid-t],\qquad
36
\widehat W=\ker\widehat C,
37
\]

38
39
and denote the last coordinate form by \(h\).  The antipodal homogenized fibre is
40

\[
42
\widehat A_t=
43
\{(x,1):x\in S_t\}\cup\{(-x,-1):x\in S_t\}
44
=\widehat W\cap\{\pm1\}^{m+1}.                         \tag{2}
45

46

Since \(C\) has row rank three,

\dim\widehat W=m-2=r+1.                                \tag{3}

Throughout, “coordinate-full” means that the displayed projective coordinate forms \(\{\widehat f_1,\ldots,\widehat
f_m,h\}\) are the entire projective full dual of \(\widehat A_t\).

## 2. Zero Gale columns can be deleted and reinserted
56

### Lemma 2.1 (one free coordinate)
58
59
Suppose \(c_1=0\), and write \(C'=(c_2,\ldots,c_m)\),
60

\[
62
S'_t=\{x'\in\{\pm1\}^{m-1}:C'x'=t\},\qquad
63
W'=\ker C'.
64

65
66
Then
67
68
\[
69
S_t=\{\pm1\}\times S'_t,qquad
70
\frac{|S_t|}{2^m}=\frac{|S'_t|}{2^{m-1}}.              \tag{4}
71

72
73
Moreover, every dimension-\(d\) residual certificate for the reduced coordinate family lifts to a dimension-\(d\)
certificate for the original family.  In particular,

\[

#### Proof
81

The factorization and density identity in (4) are immediate from \(c_1=0\).  Linearly,
83

\[

W=\mathbb Re_1\oplus W',\qquad r=1+r'.

87

\[

\dim R'=d,qquad k_{F'}(R')\le r'.

Regard \(R'\) as the subspace \(R=0\oplus R'\le W^*\).  It is spanned by the same signed differences.  The old reduced
forms occupy at most \(r'\) classes modulo \(R\), while the free coordinate \(e_1^*\) contributes at most one
additional class.  Hence

k_F(R)\le r'+1=r,

and \(R\) is a dimension-\(d\) certificate for \(F\).  This proves (5). \(\square\)

### Corollary 2.2 (arbitrarily many zero columns)
103
104
Deleting all zero Gale columns preserves the affine atom density.  If the unreduced family is projectively simple and
has \(d_{\rm geo}(F)=3\), then the reduced family also has \(d_{\rm geo}=3\).
105
106
#### Proof
107
108
Iterate Lemma 2.1.  If the reduced family admitted a residual of dimension at most two, it would lift to the unreduced
family.  Thus its geometric distance is at least three.  Its relation corank is still three, and three signed forest
edges always give a residual of dimension at most three and class excess three; hence its distance is exactly three.
\(\square\)

The other hypotheses needed by the shared-\(h\) argument also descend:

- projective simplicity is inherited by a subfamily;

- affine spanning descends under the projection \(\{\pm1\}\times S'_t\to S'_t\);

- coordinate-fullness descends through the free product.
115

For the last point, let \(A=\{\pm1\}\times A'\) span \(\mathbb R\oplus V'\), and suppose
117

\[
119
g(s,x)=as+g'(x)
120

121
122
is sign-valued on \(A\).  For every \(x\in A'\), both \(a+g'(x)\) and \(-a+g'(x)\) lie in \(\{\pm1\}\).  Therefore
either \(a=0\) and \(g'\) is sign-valued on \(A'\), or \(|a|=1\) and \(g'\) vanishes on the spanning set \(A'\), so
\(g=\pm s\).  Thus the projective full dual of a free product is exactly the disjoint union of the free coordinate and
the full dual of the reduced factor.  Consequently deleting or reinserting free coordinates creates no hidden
full-dual form.
123

This justifies every occurrence of “after zero Gale columns are deleted” in the finite reduction.

### Proposition 4.1 (forced exceptional plane)

Thus \(s\le1\) would contradict \(d_{\rm geo}(F)=3\), and (11) forces \(s=2\).
262

The homogenized rank is \(r+1\).  If an optimal plane left at most \(r\) classes, it would descend directly to a
dimension-at-most-two old residual.  The same conclusion holds if the \(h\)-class contains no old form, because
deleting \(h\) removes one class; or if \(h\in\widehat R\), because restriction loses one residual dimension and the
remaining class merger costs at most one; or if restriction causes any additional class merger.  Finally, if \(\dim
S<2\), one can adjoin one old signed difference between two remaining classes and still obtain an old residual of
dimension at most two.  Each alternative contradicts \(d_{\rm geo}(F)=3\).  Therefore all the equalities and
nondegeneracy conditions in (12)--(13) are necessary. \(\square\)

## 5. Why the exact checker is a proof certificate
304
305
The canonical verifier is
306
307
```text
308
/workspace/scratch/682eb2738f23/shared_h_exact_checker.py
309
```
310
311
It uses only Python integers and `fractions.Fraction`; it contains no numerical tolerance.  It checks three logically
separate finite claims.
312
313
### 5.1 Local three-edge relations
314
315
For the four possible three-edge forest shapes, it constructs all primitive nonzero dependence triples as cross
products of two rows in \(\{0,\pm1\}^3\).  There are exactly sixteen.  For every local zero set it computes the
projective full dual over \(\mathbb Q\), imposes projective simplicity and the global two-extra-form budget, and
verifies that every survivor is balanced on support four or six.
316
317
### 5.2 The \(m=8\) normal forms
318
319
It verifies the \((1,2,2)\) planar spectrum, enumerates all eight three-support target multisets, all twelve
\(A,B,A+B\) root configurations, and all \(A,A,B\) connected-graph constructions.  For the last family it verifies
320
321
\[
322
93{,}312\to2{,}934\to76\to20\to12.                   \tag{18}
323
\]
324
325
For every final case it constructs exact integer primal coordinate forms on \(\ker C\), enumerates signed-difference
residual lines and planes, and asserts an exact rank-at-most-two residual with at most
326
327
\[
328
r=8-3=5
329
\]
330
331
quotient classes.
332
333
### 5.3 Intrinsic closed fibres
334
335
For \(2\le R\le5\), it enumerates every nonempty antipodal subset of \(\{\pm1\}^R\).  For each spanning set, it solves
the full-dual interpolation equations over \(\mathbb Q\), closes the set by exact validity-mask intersection, retains
\(|D|=R+3\), and searches exactly for a residual line.  Thus (9) is a finite theorem, not random evidence.

## 7. Scope
371
372
This closes the exact finite and homogenization bridges for the rank-three shared-\(h\) obstruction.  It does not
prove that arbitrary high-corank singularity mechanisms decompose into these local cores; that remains a separate
global step toward the sharp iid Rademacher singularity conjecture.

Global bridge theorem, counterexamples, and precise remaining gap

# Global bridge audit for the sharp Rademacher singularity programme
2
3
## Verdict
4
5
The completed homogeneous corank-three and corank-four theorems, and the
6
target-uniform affine corank-four endpoint work, do **not** yet supply the
7
global step.  The missing deterministic statement is a linear
8
entropy--rigidity theorem for the high-corank singleton-layer cyclic core.
9
A clean sufficient form is
10

\[

13

14

16

17

18

19

20

36

37

## 5. Counterexample audit
430
431
The new theorems do not use any of the false structural shortcuts recorded
432
in the project.
433
434
1. **Products of NAE circuits.**  For \(t\) factors,
435
   \(\ell=t\log _2(4/3)\) and \(d_{\rm cb}=t\).  They force the lower
436
   constant (5), but satisfy (17) with large slack.  A two-shadow maximum
437
   matching has only linear size, whereas \(4^\ell=(4^{\beta})^t\).
438
439
2. **The rank-seven all-\((1,1)\) shared slice.**  It has
440
   \(\ell=\log _2(16/7)\) and \(d_{\rm cb}=2\), although every pairwise
441
   potential recurrence fails.  Theorems 1--2 use a simultaneous support
442
   matching and no one-pair recurrence.
443
444
3. **The \(K_{3,3}\) incremental obstruction.**  Adding the ninth form
445
   raises rigidity from one to two while deleting only one antipodal pair
446
   (ratio \(7/8\)).  No deletion-ratio assertion occurs above.  General
447
   \(K_{p,q}\) grids also satisfy (17), albeit nonsharply.
448
449
4. **Saturated-optimizer failures in \(K_{3,q}\).**  The proof never asks
450
   for a saturated geometric optimizer or a private collision.
451
452
5. **The \((3,2)\)-star/treewidth-two family.**  Its density tends to
453
   \(1/2\) while the number of ears is unbounded.  The batching proof puts
454
   all matching pairs met by the shared dense row into one batch and charges
455
   its true Littlewood--Offord atom.  Its actual residual rank remains one.
456
457
6. **Crosspolytopes.**  Their full dual has exponentially many forms, so
458
   excess-dual bounds are useless.  Here
459
   \(\ell=r-\Theta(\log r)\), so (17) is intentionally much weaker than
460
   the exact crosspolytope certificate and causes no contradiction.
Complete line-numbered archive | S6: lines 415-460

7. **Balanced middle slices and the maximum-axis-subcube failure.**  The
463
   argument uses neither an axis-aligned subcube nor a sequential rigidity
464
   increment.
465
466
8. **Arbitrary direct-sum additivity.**  The proof establishes a single
467
   global residual span and does not assume that \(d_{\rm cb}\) is additive
468
   on abstract coordinate families.
469
470
The two-shadow theorem is also consistent with a random dense row.  A row
471
of support \(s\) alone has two-point atom \(O(s^{-1/2})\), so its defect is
472
at least \((1/2)\log _2s-O(1)\), exactly the scale
473
\(s=O(4^\ell)\) appearing in (8).  Thus the exponential dependence cannot
474
be improved by a support-size argument alone; a linear theorem must use
475
basis exchange or aggregation of the residual row space.

## 6. Sharply formalized remaining gap
478
479
After Theorem 3, the unresolved deterministic statement can be confined to
480
the following class.
481
482
* The block is a singleton-layer cyclic SCC after every admissible Gale
483
  basis exchange.
484
* In the homogeneous boundary-closed case its relation corank is at least
485
  five.
486
* In the nonroot case it has genuinely affine boundary fibres and fails
487
  connector absorption.
488
* Its conditional defect satisfies the lower threshold (32), while the
489
  general basis-mass estimate forces
490
  \(\ell(n-r)<rj+O(n)\).
491
* It has no sparse-cyclomatic fundamental graph, no relation-side feedback
492
  set of size \(o(\log n)\), no large disjoint-support matching, no
493
  \(o(\log n)\) ordinary star core, no sufficiently low-rank shared form,
494
  and no multiplicative subgroup/coset fibre.
495
496
The exact theorem still required is therefore:
497
498
> **High-corank connector-rigidity theorem.**  Every such cyclic SCC has a
499
> globally compatible basis/selector certificate satisfying (3), with an
500
> absolute linear coefficient.
501
502
Equivalently, one may bypass the decomposition and prove the global
503
selector theorem (1), or the stronger Rado theorem (4), directly.  The
504
finite corank-three/four classifications verify only the first local cases
505
of this assertion.  They do not imply it by induction because one-new-form
506
ears have zero local rank-corrected entropy and because nonbinary separator

Global bridge theorem, counterexamples, and precise remaining gap
645
\(q=r-d\).  Selector feasibility says that every member of
646
\(\widetilde D(A_r)\) has the same quotient image, up to sign, as one of
647
the \(r\) members of the exchanged basis.  Therefore
648

\[
650
 |\pi(\widetilde D(A_r))|\le2r.                     \tag{44}

654

655

656

### 7.3 Consequence
666
667
The proposed inference
668
669
\[
670
 \text{``one exchanged witness per batch''}
671
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
672
 \text{residual rank }O(m)                          \tag{45}
673
\]
674
675
is false even for antipodal, spanning, fully closed real sign fibres.  In
676
the example, the one selected dense equation certifies only an
677
\(O(r^{-1/2})\) Littlewood--Offord atom, whereas the actual fibre density is
678
\(2r/2^r\).  The batching proof is intentionally allowed to leave almost
679
all of that entropy defect unused.  The unselected full-dual rows retain
680
\(r-O(\log r)\) independent quotient complexity, and triangular exchange
681
does not see it.
682
683
Thus a successful bootstrap would have to charge additional exchanges or
684
residual directions to the **remaining entropy defect** after each batch,
685
or prove a new quotient-class aggregation theorem.  This example does not
686
rule out deliberately choosing many sparse witnesses—the crosspolytope has
687
such choices with a number of batches of order \(r\).  It rules out the
688
proposed use of the entropy-efficient dense batching followed by an
689
\(O(m)\) residual claim.  Pivot triangularity supplies basis admissibility
690
and nothing stronger.

# Audit of the global exponential bridge: Theorems 1--2
2
3
## Verdict
4
5
**PASS, with two required wording/quantifier corrections.**
6
7
The weighted two-shadow compression theorem and the entropy-sharpened star-core rigidity theorem are correct.  The
triangular batching argument does give a product of conditional Littlewood--Offord factors; no independence of the
selected constraint events is being assumed.  The proof should, however, be rewritten using the disjoint sets \(U_s\)
and an explicit reverse conditional-expectation identity, as in Section 2 below.
8
9
The finite-field step is also valid for \(p>r\), but the sentence “there is no cyclic wrap” is not by itself a proof
for arbitrary coefficients in \(\mathbb F_p\).  It must invoke the finite-group Littlewood--Offord theorem: for
\(u<p\) nonzero coefficients in \(\mathbb F_p\), every one-point atom is at most \(2^{-u}\binom{u}{\lfloor
u/2\rfloor}\).  The desired two-target estimate then follows by a union bound, together with the separate \(3/4\)
two-coordinate argument.
10
11
The endpoint implication
12
13
\[
14
4^\ell=o(\ell\log n)                                  \tag{A}
15
\]
16
17
is correct when \(k=O(n/\log n)\) and \(\ell\to\infty\).  The displayed lower threshold for a “survivor” needs a
subsequence or pointwise-ratio quantifier: failure of a little-\(o\) condition only guarantees the threshold along an
infinite subsequence on which \(4^\ell/(\ell\log n)\ge c>0\), not necessarily at every index of an oscillating
sequence.
18
19
No dependency, exposure, Littlewood--Offord, or selector-rank counterexample was found.
20

## 2. Correct triangular batching and product conditioning
48
49
Let \(P_1,\ldots,P_N\) be a maximum matching of the two-shadow graph.  At stage \(s\), choose a still-present matching
edge, choose a row \(E_s\) containing that edge, and remove
50
51
\[
52
B_s=\{u:P_u\text{ is still present and }P_u\cap E_s\ne\varnothing\},
53
\qquad a_s=|B_s|.                                     \tag{4}
54
\]
55
56
Let
57
58
\[
59
V_s=\bigcup_{u\in B_s}P_u,qquad U_s=E_s\cap V_s.     \tag{5}
60
\]
61
62
The proof uses \(U_s\), not all of \(V_s\).  The following facts are exact.
63
64
1. The batches \(B_s\) partition the matching edges, so the \(V_s\)'s are disjoint.
65
2. The \(U_s\)'s are therefore pairwise disjoint.
66
3. The witness edge contributes both endpoints to \(U_s\), while each of the other \(a_s-1\) matching edges in \(B_s\)
contributes at least one distinct endpoint.  Hence
67
   \[
68
   |U_s|\ge a_s+1.                                    \tag{6}
69
   \]
70
4. If \(t<s\), the matching edges in \(B_s\) were still present at stage \(t\).  Had \(E_t\) met an endpoint of one of
them, that edge would have been removed at stage \(t\).  Consequently
71
   \[
72
   E_t\cap U_s=\varnothing\qquad(t<s).               \tag{7}
73
   \]
74
5. Row \(E_s\) meets no endpoint belonging to a later batch; otherwise that still-present edge would belong to
\(B_s\).  Thus, after coordinates outside \(\bigcup_sU_s\) are fixed, the indicator of the \(s\)-th selected
constraint is a function only of
75
   \[
76
   X_{U_1},\ldots,X_{U_s}.                            \tag{8}

## 3. Two-target Littlewood--Offord quantifiers
129
130
After all parameters outside a selected active set of size \(u\) are fixed, the event has exactly the form
131
132
\[
133
\sum_{i=1}^{u}c_i\varepsilon_i\in\{b-1,b+1\},
134
\qquad c_i\ne0,                                      \tag{12}
135
\]
136
137
for an arbitrary \(b\) depending on the fixed variables.  The bound must therefore be uniform in \(b\), in the
coefficients, and in all earlier block values.
138
139
### 3.1 The \(3/4\) bound
140
141
For any two active coordinates, condition on the other \(u-2\) signs.  The four sums
142
143
\[
144
\pm c_1\pm c_2
145
\]
146
147
cannot all lie in a fixed two-point set when \(c_1,c_2\ne0\) over \(\mathbb R\), or in the present finite-field range.
 Indeed, the four values have fewer than three distinct elements only if \(c_1=\pm c_2\); in that case they are
\(0,2c_1,-2c_1\) up to translation, still three distinct values over \(\mathbb R\) and over \(\mathbb F_p\) with odd
\(p>2\).  Hence at most three of the four assignments work, and
148
149
\[
150
\Pr\left(\sum c_i\varepsilon_i\in\{b-1,b+1\}\right)\le\frac34. \tag{13}
151
\]
152
153
This argument is target-uniform and works in every odd characteristic.

source page 5/11
Independent audit of weighted two-shadow compression

155
### 3.2 The two one-point atoms
156
157
Over \(\mathbb R\), the classical Littlewood--Offord theorem gives
158
159
\[
160
\sup_y\Pr\left(\sum_{i=1}^{u}c_i\varepsilon_i=y\right)
161
\le 2^{-u}{u\choose\lfloor u/2\rfloor}.              \tag{14}
162
\]
163
164
Over \(\mathbb F_p\), the same inequality holds for nonzero coefficients when \(u<p\); this is the finite-group
Littlewood--Offord theorem in the no-wrap range (for example, the \(\mathbb F_p\) specialization of the optimal group
inequality of JuškeviIius--Šemetulskis).  In the present application,
165
166
\[
167
u\le r<p.                                             \tag{15}
168
\]
169
170
Since \(p\) is odd, the two targets in (12) are distinct.  Applying (14) to each target and taking a union bound gives
171
172
\[
173
\Pr\left(\sum c_i\varepsilon_i\in\{b-1,b+1\}\right)
174
\le 2^{1-u}{u\choose\lfloor u/2\rfloor}.             \tag{16}
175
\]
176
177
Combining (13) and (16) proves exactly
178
179
\[
180
\psi(u)=\min\left\{\frac34,
181
2^{1-u}{u\choose\lfloor u/2\rfloor}\right\}.         \tag{17}
182
\]
183
184
Thus the quantifiers in the batching proof are correct.  The required correction is expository: “\(u<p\), so there is
no cyclic wrap” should be replaced by an explicit invocation of the finite-group one-point theorem.  Arbitrary field
coefficients can wrap numerically; what is true is that the optimal finite-group concentration theorem has the
central-binomial value when every nonzero coefficient has order \(p>u\).
185
186

## 5. Audit of entropy-sharpened star-core rigidity
240
241
The proof of Theorem 2 is correct.
242
243
For each \(j\notin C\), call \(j\) free if some realized core pattern \(X_C=c\) admits points of \(S\) with both signs
of \(X_j\).  A row using \(j\) has the form
244
245
\[
246
h_a(X)=q_a(X_C)+\lambda_aX_j,qquad\lambda_a\ne0.      \tag{23}
247
\]
248
249
Evaluating at the two points with the same core and opposite \(X_j\) gives
250
251
\[
252
q_a(c)+\lambda_a, q_a(c)-\lambda_a\in\{\pm1\}.
253
\]
254
255
They are distinct in characteristic zero or odd characteristic, so
256
257
\[
258
q_a(c)=0,qquad\lambda_a=\pm1.                        \tag{24}
259
\]
260
261
Assign the row to \(\lambda_aX_j\); its residual is supported on \(C\).
262
263
Let \(N_0\) be the set of never-free outside coordinates.  For each realized \(c\), every coordinate \(X_j\), \(j\in
N_0\), has a unique sign.  Hence the entire vector is a function of the core:
264
265
\[
266
H(X_{N_0}\mid X_C)=0.                                \tag{25}
267
\]
Complete line-numbered archive | S7: lines 230-267
134 / 200

S7  audit_global_exponential_report.md
source page 8/11
Independent audit of weighted two-shadow compression
268
269
For uniform \(X\) on \(S\),
270
271
\[
272
\begin{aligned}
273
r-\ell=H(X)
274
&\le H(X_C,X_{N_0})+r-|C|-|N_0|\\
275
&=H(X_C)+r-|C|-|N_0|,
276
\end{aligned}                                         \tag{26}
277
\]
278
279
so
280
281
\[
282
|N_0|\le\ell-(|C|-H(X_C))\le\ell.                   \tag{27}
283
\]
284
285
Rows using a never-free \(j\) may be assigned to either signed copy of \(X_j\), leaving a residual supported on
\(C\cup\{j\}\).  A core-only row is assigned to any signed coordinate in \(C\), leaving a residual supported on \(C\).
 The edge case \(C=\varnothing\) should be stated explicitly: then a noncoordinate row of support at least two would
create a two-shadow edge and a nonempty matching, so no such core-only row exists.  Discarded coordinate rows assign
to themselves and have zero residual.
286
287
All residuals consequently lie in
288
289
\[
290
\operatorname{span}\{e_i:i\in C\cup N_0\},
291
\]
292
293
and therefore
294
295
\[
296
d(H)\le |C|+|N_0|\le9\,4^\ell+\ell.                 \tag{28}
297
\]
298
299
For a full-closed fibre, taking the rows to be its entire projective full dual makes their compatibility set exactly
\(S\).  Adding the input basis rows costs zero residual rank, and optimizing over coordinate bases gives the asserted
\(d_{\rm cb}\) bound.  No additional closure hypothesis is used inside the fixed-basis argument.
300
301
Antipodality is harmless and in fact automatic for a nonempty compatibility set defined by homogeneous two-sided
constraints \(h_a\cdot x\in\{\pm1\}\).

## 7. Endpoint implication and its exact quantifier
337
338
The component exponent is
339
340
\[
341
-(n-r)\ell+O\bigl((4^\ell+\ell)k+j\log r+r+n\bigr).   \tag{29}

Assume

346
\[
347
k=O(n/\log n),\qquad \ell\to\infty,qquad
348
4^\ell=o(\ell\log n).                                \tag{30}
349
\]
350
351
Since \(r,j\le k\),
352
353
\[
354
n-r=n(1-o(1)),qquad
355
j\log r+r+n=O(n).                                    \tag{31}
356
\]
357
358
Moreover,
359

\[
361
\frac{(4^\ell+\ell)k}{(n-r)\ell}
362

363
\]
364
365
and
366
367
\[
368
\frac{j\log r+r+n}{(n-r)\ell}=O(1/\ell)=o(1).        \tag{33}
369
\]
370
371
Thus (29) equals
372
373
\[
374
-(1-o(1))(n-r)\ell,                                  \tag{34}
375

377
which verifies the claimed exponential component saving.

To translate failure of (30) into a lower threshold, use a pointwise hypothesis.  If for some fixed \(c>0\)

\]

If a sequence merely fails \(4^\ell=o(\ell\log n)\), there exist \(c>0\) and an infinite subsequence on which (35),
and therefore (37), holds.  An oscillating sequence need not satisfy (37) at every index.  The sentence surrounding

406

## 8. Exact corrections recommended
412
413
1. Replace the reverse-exposure paragraph in Theorem 1 by equations (5)--(11), explicitly using \(U_s\) and
conditional expectation.
414
2. Replace “the finite-field statement is valid because \(u<p\), so there is no cyclic wrap” by the precise
finite-group one-point Littlewood--Offord lemma (14)--(15), followed by the two-target union bound.
415
3. Mention that when \(|U_s|>a_s+1\), the extra active coordinates are conditioned before applying the
\((a_s+1)\)-term bound.
416
4. Separate the trivial \(N=0\) case before the weighted sum (20).
417
5. In Theorem 2, state how core-only rows are assigned, including the \(C=\varnothing\) edge case.
418
6. Qualify the lower-threshold conclusion as a pointwise statement under (35), or as an infinite-subsequence
conclusion when only failure of little-\(o\) is known.
419
420
With these corrections, Theorems 1--2 and the controlled endpoint range are rigorous.  They remain exponential in
\(\ell\) and therefore do not close the high-corank connector-rigidity problem identified elsewhere in the report.

def maximum_matching(r: int, edges: set[tuple[int, int]]) -> tuple[tuple[int, int], ...]:
27
    adjacency = [0] * r
28
    for i, j in edges:
29
        adjacency[i] |= 1 << j

        adjacency[j] |= 1 << i

    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)

    def solve(mask: int) -> tuple[tuple[int, int], ...]:

        if not mask:

37
        v = vbit.bit_length() - 1

def make_batches(rows: tuple[tuple[int, ...], ...],
53
                 matching: tuple[tuple[int, int], ...]) -> list[tuple[int, tuple[int, ...], frozenset[int]]]:
54
    """One canonical allowed greedy batching: (witness row, pair ids, U_s)."""
55
    supports = [support(row) for row in rows]
56
    remaining = set(range(len(matching)))
57
    batches = []

        witness = min(i for i, e in enumerate(supports) if pair <= e)

66
        batches.append((witness, batch, us))

        remaining.difference_update(batch)
68
    return batches

def rational_rank(rows: list[tuple[int, ...]]) -> int:

    if not rows:

    a = [[Fraction(x) for x in row] for row in rows]

    rr = 0

99

100

def finite_field_lo_search() -> int:
242
    primes = (3, 5, 7, 11, 13)
243
    classes = 0
244
    for u in range(2, 9):
245
        for p in primes:
246
            if p <= u:
247
                continue
248
            # Sign changes and permutations preserve the distribution, so
249
            # coefficient multisets in {1,...,(p-1)/2} are exhaustive.
250
            for coeff in combinations_with_replacement(range(1, (p + 1) // 2), u):
251
                frequency = [0] * p
252
                for signs in product((-1, 1), repeat=u):
253
                    frequency[sum(a * x for a, x in zip(coeff, signs)) % p] += 1
254
                worst = max(frequency[(center - 1) % p] +
255
                            frequency[(center + 1) % p]
256
                            for center in range(p))
257
                bound = min(3 * (1 << u) // 4, 2 * comb(u, u // 2))
258
                assert worst <= bound, (u, p, coeff, worst, bound)
259
                classes += 1
260
    return classes

1
# Structured linear rigidity after weighted two-shadow exchange
2
3
## Verdict
4
5
There is a rigorous linear-in-defect theorem for the part of the
6
high-corank problem in which the weighted two-shadow batching has bounded
7
**entropy-normalized exposure congestion**.  It is conditional, works on
8
affine boundary fibres, uses the conditional defect which the dense-witness
9
exchange leaves unused, and controls all unselected rows.  In the original
10
junction basis its constant is
11
12
\[
13
 2\kappa+1,
14
\]
15
16
where \(\kappa\) is the maximum number of matching pairs removed by a batch
17
divided by that batch's Littlewood--Offord entropy charge.  If the selected
18
witnesses have private support at most \(s\), then
19
20
\[
21
 \kappa\le {s-1\over \beta},\qquad
22
 \beta=\log _2(4/3),
23
\]
24
25
and hence the new quotient residual rank is at most
26
27
\[
28
 \left(1+{2(s-1)\over\beta}\right)L.              \tag{1}
29
\]
30
31
If one insists on using the triangularly exchanged witness basis, the
32
constant increases by only \(\beta^{-1}\):
33
34
\[
35
 \left(1+{2(s-1)\over\beta}+{1\over\beta}\right)L
36
 =\left(1+{2s-1\over\beta}\right)L.               \tag{2}
37
\]
38
39
This is a genuine residual-compression result, not the false inference
40
that one residual direction is enough per selected witness.
41
42
Connector absorption does not follow from bounded support, overlap, or
43
incidence degree.  A fully closed spanning antipodal affine star with
44
private support one and private incidence degree three has no absorbing
Complete line-numbered archive | S9: lines 1-44
