Bounded-congestion linear rigidity and connector criterion
45
signed-selector certificate for **any** boundary-preserving admissible sign
46
basis.  Thus a connector compatibility hypothesis is mathematically
47
necessary.  The exact useful hypothesis is a kernel/factorization
48
certificate: every linear dependency among the private projections of the
49
chosen residuals must have boundary trace already in the ancestor residual
50
space.  Under this hypothesis, (1), or (2) after exchange, gives the full
51
rooted estimate (3) of `global_bridge_report.md`.
52
53
The resulting theorem covers bounded-support cyclic blocks whose connector
54
labels close on a fundamental-cycle basis (in particular, scaled graphic or
55
network residual systems with paid cycle sums).  It does not solve the
56
remaining dense, noncoherent singleton-layer SCC.
57
58
## 1. Rooted setup and the structural parameter
59
60
Let \(\mathbb K=\mathbb R\), or \(\mathbb K=\mathbb F_p\) with odd
61
\(p>q\).  Let \((W,Q)\) be a sign basis on a finite support \(A\), where
62
\(Q=(Q_1,\ldots,Q_q)\) is the private part.  Write
63
64
\[
65
 B=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb K}W,
66
 \qquad R_<\subseteq B,
67
 \qquad L=q-H(Q\mid W).                            \tag{3}
68
\]
69
70
Let the private forms be
71
72
\[
73
 Y_a=u_a(W)+h_a(Q),\qquad Y_a(A)\subseteq\{\pm1\}, \tag{4}
74
\]
75
76
and put \(E_a=\operatorname{supp}h_a\).  Boundary-only forms are passed to
77
the parent.  Singleton private supports are retained, but do not create
78
two-shadow edges.
79
80
Form the private two-shadow graph \(\Gamma\): \(ij\) is an edge when some
81
\(E_a\) contains \(i,j\).  Fix a maximum matching
82
\(P_1,\ldots,P_N\).  Run the weighted batching from Theorem 1 of
83
`global_bridge_report.md`.  At batch \(s\), a witness support \(E_s\)
84
removes \(a_s\ge1\) still-present matching pairs.  Put
85
86
\[
87
 \psi(u)=\min\left\{\frac34,
88
  2^{1-u}{u\choose\lfloor u/2\rfloor}\right\},

89
 \qquad c_s=-\log _2\psi(a_s+1).                  \tag{5}
90
\]
91
92
Thus \(c_s\ge\beta=\log _2(4/3)\).  Define the exposure congestion of
93
this batching by
94
95
\[
96
 \kappa(\mathcal B)=
97
 \max_s {a_s\over c_s};                            \tag{6}
98
\]
99
100
when \(N=0\), set \(\kappa(\mathcal B)=0\).  One can minimize (6) over
101
the choice of maximum matching, witness row, and greedy order, but the
102
theorem needs only one displayed batching certificate.
103
104
This parameter is strictly more flexible than a row-support bound.  Two
105
immediate sufficient hypotheses are:
106
107
* if every selected witness meets at most \(D\) still-present matching
108
  pairs, then \(\kappa\le D/\beta\);
109
* if every selected witness has private support at most \(s\), then
110
  \(a_s+1\le |E_s|\le s\), hence
111
  \(\kappa\le(s-1)/\beta\).
112
113
In particular, maximum private row support \(s\) is sufficient.  Also, if
114
the two-shadow graph has maximum degree \(\Delta\), each row support spans
115
a clique and has size at most \(\Delta+1\), giving
116
\(\kappa\le\Delta/\beta\).
117

## 2. Conditional linear quotient theorem
119
120
### Theorem 1 (bounded-congestion residual compression)
121
122
In the setup above, a coordinate-selector certificate \(R\) exists such
123
that
124
125
\[
126
 \boxed{
127
  \dim {R+B\over B}\le(2\kappa+1)L .}             \tag{7}
128
\]
129
130
Here \(\kappa\) can be the congestion of any one certified batching.  No
131
full-closure or antipodality hypothesis is needed.
132

### Proof
134
135
For a realized boundary value \(w\), let \(A_w\) be the conditional
136
private support.  Conditional uniformity gives
137
138
\[
139
 L=\mathbb E_w\bigl[q-\log _2|A_w|\bigr].         \tag{8}
140
\]

* **Private anchors.**  If the matrix of private residual projections has

  private anchors proves this immediately.

Bounded-congestion linear rigidity and connector criterion
440
take its one private member to be either \(q\) or one \(Y_j\), up to sign.
441
442
If it takes \(q\), choose any two of the \(Y_i\).  If it takes \(Y_j\),
443
choose the other two.  Call them \(Y_i,Y_k\).  For an arbitrary signed
444
basis selector \(g\), the residual \(Y_i\mp g\) retains at least one of
445
the two private-to-that-row boundary coordinates \(x_i,z_i\).  A residual
446
from \(Y_k\) can meet at most one of \(x_i,z_i\), since a basis selector
447
has at most one such boundary coordinate (and the sole nonboundary basis
448
form is supported on the \(j\)-th pair).  If the two residuals were
449
proportional, the first would therefore have to cancel one of its own pair
450
coordinates by selecting that coordinate.  It would then have no support
451
on \(x_k,z_k\), whereas the second residual necessarily retains at least
452
one of them.  This is a contradiction.
453
454
Thus the two residuals are linearly independent for every selector choice.
455
Their span has dimension at least two, but its private projection has
456
dimension at most one.  It consequently contains a nonzero pure-boundary
457
vector.  With \(R_<=0\), connector absorption is impossible.
458
459
This example is a low-rank star, not one of the final cyclic SCC survivors.
460
Its role is logical: bounded private row support, bounded private incidence
461
degree, conditional defect, and basis optimization do **not** imply
462
connector absorption.  A cycle-kernel/common-lift condition, or some
463
stronger structural replacement for it, is indispensable.
464
465
## 6. Integration consequence
466
467
The valid replacement for the residual-compression step is therefore:
468
469
1. exhibit a maximum-matching batching with bounded \(\kappa\) (bounded
470
   witness support, matching overlap, or two-shadow degree suffices);
471
2. use Theorem 1 to charge the entire unselected residual quotient to the
472
   conditional defect \(L\);
473
3. if the witness exchange is required, add its at most \(L/\beta\)
474
   discrepancy directions;
475
4. verify the connector kernel on a dependency basis, preferably a
476
   fundamental-cycle basis in a network-type cyclic block.
477
478
Under these explicit hypotheses, the block satisfies the rooted linear
479
estimate needed by the recursive telescope.  Without step 4, even
480
support-one, degree-three fully closed fibres fail the absorption statement.
481
The remaining open problem is to derive such coherence, or an alternative
482
global aggregation mechanism, for the genuinely dense high-corank cyclic
483
SCCs rather than assume it.

## Verdict
4
5

## 3. Transfer from real signed classes to the binary quotient

## 7. Consequence for the remaining global strategy
464
465
The result rules out the following proposed shortcut:
466
467
> decompose the full dual into three-connected matroid blocks, and charge
468
> every three-connected block with the ceiling estimate
469
> \(d_{\rm cb}\le\lceil\ell\rceil\).
470
471
The rooted NAE cycles are already single three-connected blocks, and the
472
failure is extensive rather than an isolated finite anomaly: their exact
473
geometric deficit is \(n/3+O(1)\), while their density defect is
474
\((1-\log _2\varphi)n+o(1)\).
475
476
This also clarifies what the weighted two-shadow batching/basis-exchange
477
route would have to prove.  One residual direction can account for three
478
quotient mergers in this family, exactly as in (19), and those directions
479
must be paid at positive entropy cost throughout the cyclic block.  Merely
480
invoking three-connectivity or maximal Rado transversality cannot supply
481
that payment.  The family remains compatible with a constant-factor
482
conditional-counting or activation theorem, so it is not a counterexample
483
to the desired global \(O(\ell)\) conclusion.

## 3. Real-matroid three-connectivity, including \(b=0,n\)

<\frac n3=d_{\rm geo}(A_n)\le d_{\rm cb}(A_n).

\]
292
293
At the first member,

so the numerical endpoint agrees with the uniform inequality.

## Final assessment

**PASS.**  The rooted-NAE cycles are valid full-closed, genuinely

# Exact selector and activation certificates for rooted NAE cycles
2
3
## Result
4
5
For the rooted NAE cycle fibre
6
7
\[
8
A_n=\{(a,b_1,\ldots,b_n)\in\{\pm1\}^{n+1}:
9
       a+b_i+b_{i+1}\in\{\pm1\}\text{ for every }i\},
10
\qquad n\ge4,
11
\]
12
13
the geometric result in `rigidity_attack_report.md` can be sharpened to
14

\[
16
\boxed{d_{\rm cb}(A_n)=d_{\rm geo}(A_n)=\left\lceil\frac n3\right\rceil.}
17
\tag{1}
18

19
20
Moreover, the optimal selector certificate satisfies the full activation-cell

\[

|A_{n,z}|\le 2^{r-d-|z|},
25
\qquad r=n+1,\quad d=\left\lceil\frac n3\right\rceil,
26
\tag{2}
27

28
29
and is connector-transverse when the root sign (a) is treated as the
30

31

32

The family has rank (r=n+1).  The lower bound in (1) is already proved

## 2. A minimum independent dominating set
59
60
Put (d=\lceil n/3\rceil).  There is an independent dominating set
61
(U\subseteq V(C_n)) of size (d) whose cyclic gaps have length two or
62

63
64
\[

\begin{array}{c|c}
66
n&\text{cyclic gap lengths}\\ \hline
67
3k&3,\ldots,3,\\
68
3k+1&2,2,3,\ldots,3,\\
69

70

\tag{5}

\tag{6}

The (L_u)'s are linearly independent because the (B_u)-coordinate
83

The exact Lucas-number density is stronger asymptotically, but (15) is the

## 5. Connector compatibility
224
225
Treat (A=e_0) as the incoming boundary coordinate and the (B_i=e_i)
226

227

## 6. Scope
252
253
The construction solves a genuine infinite family of three-connected,
254
high-corank cyclic blocks.  It does not prove the universal linear theorem:
255
the key extra structure is the independent dominating set whose short
256
gaps simultaneously provide
257
258
1. one residual direction per three cycle vertices;
259
2. a full-dual coordinate transversal; and
260
3. a product activation profile.
261
262
The remaining global problem is to replace these short-gap witnesses by an
263
entropy-weighted analogue in an arbitrary singleton-layer cyclic block.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
2
"""Exact checks for rooted_nae_selector_activation.md.
3
4
Only integer arithmetic and fractions.Fraction are used.  The structural
5
checks run through a requested range of n.  The activation check enumerates
6
independent sets and is therefore kept to moderate n.
7
"""
8
9
from __future__ import annotations
10
11
import argparse
12
from fractions import Fraction
13
from itertools import product
14
15
16
def rank(rows: list[tuple[int, ...]]) -> int:
17
    if not rows:
18
        return 0
19
    a = [[Fraction(x) for x in row] for row in rows]
20
    m, n = len(a), len(a[0])
21
    out = 0
22
    for col in range(n):
23
        pivot = next((i for i in range(out, m) if a[i][col]), None)
24
        if pivot is None:
25
            continue
26
        a[out], a[pivot] = a[pivot], a[out]
27
        z = a[out][col]
28
        a[out] = [x / z for x in a[out]]
29
        for i in range(m):
30
            if i != out and a[i][col]:
31
                z = a[i][col]
32
                a[i] = [x - z * y for x, y in zip(a[i], a[out])]
33
        out += 1
34
        if out == m:
35
            break
36
    return out

# Exact selector distance and connector certificates for rooted NAE cycles
2
3
## Verdict
4
5
For the rooted NAE cycle fibre from `rigidity_attack_report.md`, the
6
geometric lower bound is attained by an actual selector basis:
7
8
\[
9
\boxed{d_{\rm cb}(A_n)=d_{\rm geo}(A_n)
10
=\left\lceil\frac n3\right\rceil}
11
\qquad(n\ge4).
12
\tag{1}
13
\]
14
15
The proof below gives the coordinate basis and every selector explicitly.
16
It also gives a target-uniform activation-cell certificate of the same
17
dimension.
18
19
There are two useful connector consequences.
20
21
1. If the common root sign \(A\) is incoming boundary and the \(n\) cycle
22
   signs are private, the residual space has zero intersection with the
23
   boundary.  Its new rank is at most twice the private conditional defect:
24
25
   \[
26
   \dim R=\left\lceil\frac n3\right\rceil
27
   \le 2\bigl(n-H(B_1,\ldots,B_n\mid W)\bigr).
28
   \tag{2}
29
   \]
30
31
   Thus rooted NAE cycles are connector-compatible cyclic SCCs, with
32
   constant 2, whenever the only shared cycle coordinate is the
33
   homogenizing root (additional disjoint ancestor coordinates are
34
   harmless).
35
36
2. For an interval with \(m\) private vertices and its root and two
37
   endpoints in the incoming boundary, there is an explicit boundary-
38
   preserving selector basis of new residual rank
39
40
   \[
41
   d_m=\left\lceil\frac{m+1}{3}\right\rceil,
42
   \qquad R_m\cap\operatorname{span}W=0.
43
   \tag{3}
44
   \]

   For every \(m\ge2\),

## 7. The one-vertex connector obstruction
658
659
Let \(m=1\), with boundary vertices \(0,2\) and private vertex \(1\).
660
For the neutral boundary state, both signs of \(B_1\) occur, so its private
661
defect is zero.  This can dominate a spanning full-closed fibre, not merely
662
an isolated nonspanning boundary state.
663
664
Indeed, attach \(k\) further stable-set vertices \(z_1,\ldots,z_k\), each
665
adjacent to both boundary vertices \(0,2\), and to no other vertex.  In the
666
positive half, the neutral endpoint pattern has \(2^{k+1}\) extensions:
667
both \(B_1\) and all \(z_j\)'s are free.  Each of the other three endpoint
668
patterns has one extension.  The homogenized stable-set fibre is spanning
669
and full closed, and
670
671
\[
672
H(B_1\mid A,B_0,B_2)
673
=\frac{2^{k+1}}{2^{k+1}+3},
674
\qquad
675
L_{Q\mid W}=\frac{3}{2^{k+1}+3}\longrightarrow0.
676
\tag{63}
677
\]
678
679
Nevertheless the local path has exact selector distance one.  With no
680
prepaid boundary residual, connector absorption forces a nonzero new
681
direction, so no inequality
682
683
\[
684
\dim\frac{R+R_<}{R_<}\le C L_{Q\mid W}
685
\tag{64}
686
\]
687
688
can hold with an absolute \(C\).
689
690
There is an exact absorbed alternative.  Use the coordinate basis
691
692
\[
693
\{A,B_0,B_1,B_2\}
694
\tag{65}
695
\]
696
697
and assign both edge forms to \(B_1\):
698
699
\[

715

716

## 8. Consequences for cyclic SCC decompositions
719
720
The results separate three phenomena cleanly.
721
722
* A closed rooted NAE cycle with the homogenizer in the boundary is a
723
  genuinely cyclic, connector-compatible block: its optimal selector rank
724
  is \(\lceil n/3\rceil\), its residual space has no boundary intersection,
725
  and its conditional defect pays that rank with coefficient 2.
726
727
* Cutting the cycle to an interval preserves an exact boundary-containing
728
  selector basis.  Every interval of at least two private vertices has a
729
  uniform conditional linear bound; all but the three-vertex interval have
730
  the stronger activation-cell certificate.
731
732
* A one-private-vertex interval cannot be charged locally by conditional
733
  defect.  It must either pass its boundary residuals to an ancestor or be
734
  handled by the global triangular activation budget.  Treating it as an
735
  independent cyclic core would repeat precisely the connector direction
736
  which condition (14) of `recursive_paired_block_tree_entropy.md` was
737
  designed to absorb.
738
739
Thus rooted NAE cycles themselves are not an obstruction to the desired
740
connector theorem.  Their only boundary pathology occurs after cutting
741
them into an acyclic one-vertex interval, and that pathology is exactly
742
resolved by ancestor residual absorption rather than by a new cyclic
